```
   454→// PostCommit is called by the git post-commit hook after a commit is created.
   455→// Uses the session state machine to determine what action to take per session:
   456→//   - ACTIVE → condense immediately (each commit gets its own checkpoint)
   457→//   - IDLE → condense immediately
   458→//   - ENDED → condense if files touched, discard if empty
   459→//
   460→// After condensation for ACTIVE sessions, remaining uncommitted files are
   461→// carried forward to a new shadow branch so the next commit gets its own checkpoint.
   462→//
   463→// Shadow branches are only deleted when ALL sessions sharing the branch are non-active
   464→// and were condensed during this PostCommit.
   465→// During rebase/cherry-pick/revert operations, phase transitions are skipped entirely.
   466→//
   467→//nolint:unparam,maintidx // error return required by interface but hooks must return nil; maintidx: complex but already well-structured
   468→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) PostCommit() error {
   469→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   470→
   471→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
   472→	if err != nil {
   473→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   474→	}
   475→
   476→	// Get HEAD commit to check for trailer
   477→	head, err := repo.Head()
   478→	if err != nil {
   479→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   480→	}
   481→
   482→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(head.Hash())
   483→	if err != nil {
   484→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   485→	}
   486→
   487→	// Check if commit has checkpoint trailer (ParseCheckpoint validates format)
   488→	checkpointID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(commit.Message)
   489→	if !found {
   490→		// No trailer — user removed it or it was never added (mid-turn commit).
   491→		// Still update BaseCommit for active sessions so future commits can match.
   492→		s.postCommitUpdateBaseCommitOnly(logCtx, head)
   493→		return nil
   494→	}
   495→
   496→	worktreePath, err := GetWorktreePath()
   497→	if err != nil {
   498→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   499→	}
   500→
   501→	// Find all active sessions for this worktree
   502→	sessions, err := s.findSessionsForWorktree(worktreePath)
   503→	if err != nil || len(sessions) == 0 {
   504→		logging.Warn(logCtx, "post-commit: no active sessions despite trailer",
   505→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   506→			slog.String("checkpoint_id", checkpointID.String()),
   507→		)
   508→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Intentional: hooks must be silent on failure
   509→	}
   510→
   511→	// Build transition context
   512→	isRebase := isGitSequenceOperation()
   513→	transitionCtx := session.TransitionContext{
   514→		IsRebaseInProgress: isRebase,
   515→	}
   516→
   517→	if isRebase {
   518→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "post-commit: rebase/sequence in progress, skipping phase transitions",
   519→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   520→		)
   521→	}
   522→
   523→	// Track shadow branch names and whether they can be deleted
```

Looking at the log timeline more carefully:

```
15:05:52 - prepare-commit-msg: trailer added (71714d3bd362)
           (PostCommit should fire here, but NO log entry)
15:05:58 - post-task fires
15:06:01 - stop hook fires
```

The commit was made by a **subagent** (inside a Task). The subagent is running in a subprocess. Let me check — does the git post-commit hook even get called when the subagent makes a commit? The subagent uses `git commit` which should trigger the hook... but the subagent's environment might not have the hook installed, or the hook might fail silently.

Let me check how the git hooks are installed.

The `GetWorktreePath()` in PostCommit should resolve to the repo's working directory. But what if the subagent is running from a different cwd?

Actually, wait. The more fundamental issue is: the commit happens at `15:05:52`, but the **subagent is still running** at that point (inside the Task tool). The post-commit hook fires, calls `PostCommit()`, which tries to condense. But the session state was written during `InitializeSession` at prompt time. The BaseCommit would be the parent of this commit (`fe1c1c39`).

Let me re-examine: the shadow branch name is `entire/8b56120-e3b0c4`. `8b56120` is the first 7 chars of the commit the subagent just made (`8b56120cab78addc...`). But at the time PostCommit runs, HEAD is now `8b56120`, and `state.BaseCommit` was `fe1c1c39` (the initial commit). The shadow branch name would be `getShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit, state.WorktreeID)` = `entire/fe1c1c3-<worktreeHash>`.

But the Stop hook later creates `entire/8b56120-e3b0c4`. That means at Stop time, BaseCommit was already updated to `8b56120`. So either PostCommit updated it, or the Stop hook's `migrateAndPersistIfNeeded` did.

The PostCommit handler at line 544 checks `state.Phase.IsActive()` — if StepCount is 0 and FilesTouched is empty, it falls through to `sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles`.    702→// condenseAndUpdateState runs condensation for a session and updates state afterward.
   703→// Returns true if condensation succeeded.
   704→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) condenseAndUpdateState(
   705→	logCtx context.Context,
   706→	repo *git.Repository,
   707→	checkpointID id.CheckpointID,
   708→	state *SessionState,
   709→	head *plumbing.Reference,
   710→	shadowBranchName string,
   711→	shadowBranchesToDelete map[string]struct{},
   712→) bool {
   713→	result, err := s.CondenseSession(repo, checkpointID, state)
   714→	if err != nil {
   715→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Warning: condensation failed for session %s: %v\n",
   716→			state.SessionID, err)
   717→		logging.Warn(logCtx, "post-commit: condensation failed",
   718→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   719→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   720→		)
   721→		return false
   722→	}
   723→
   724→	// Track this shadow branch for cleanup
   725→	shadowBranchesToDelete[shadowBranchName] = struct{}{}
   726→

   729→	state.BaseCommit = newHead
   730→	state.AttributionBaseCommit = newHead
   731→	state.StepCount = 0
   732→	state.CheckpointTranscriptStart = result.TotalTranscriptLines
   733→
   734→	// Clear attribution tracking — condensation already used these values
   735→	state.PromptAttributions = nil
   736→	state.PendingPromptAttribution = nil
   737→	state.FilesTouched = nil
   738→
   739→	// Save checkpoint ID so subsequent commits can reuse it (e.g., amend restores trailer)

   743→	if len(shortID) > 8 {

   385→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) extractSessionDataFromLiveTranscript(state *SessionState) (*ExtractedSessionData, error) {
   386→	data := &ExtractedSessionData{}
   387→
   388→	// Read the live transcript
   389→	if state.TranscriptPath == "" {
   390→		return nil, errors.New("no transcript path in session state")
   391→	}
   392→
   393→	liveData, err := os.ReadFile(state.TranscriptPath)
   394→	if err != nil {
   395→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read live transcript: %w", err)
   396→	}
   397→
   398→	if len(liveData) == 0 {
   399→		return nil, errors.New("live transcript is empty")
   400→	}
   401→
   402→	fullTranscript := string(liveData)
   403→	data.Transcript = liveData
   404→	data.FullTranscriptLines = countTranscriptItems(state.AgentType, fullTranscript)
   405→	data.Prompts = extractUserPrompts(state.AgentType, fullTranscript)
   406→	data.Context = generateContextFromPrompts(data.Prompts)
   407→
   408→	// Extract files from transcript since state.FilesTouched may be empty for mid-session commits
   409→	// (no SaveChanges/Stop has been called yet to populate it)
   410→	if len(state.FilesTouched) > 0 {
   411→		data.FilesTouched = state.FilesTouched
   412→	} else {
   413→		// Extract modified files from transcript
   414→		ag, agErr := agent.GetByAgentType(state.AgentType)
   415→		if agErr == nil {
   416→			if analyzer, ok := ag.(agent.TranscriptAnalyzer); ok {
   417→				modifiedFiles, _, extractErr := analyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(state.TranscriptPath, state.CheckpointTranscriptStart)
   418→				if extractErr == nil && len(modifiedFiles) > 0 {
   419→					// Normalize to repo-relative paths
   420→					basePath := state.WorktreePath
   421→					if basePath == "" {
   422→						if wp, wpErr := GetWorktreePath(); wpErr == nil {
   423→							basePath = wp
   424→						}
   425→					}
   426→					if basePath != "" {
   427→						normalized := make([]string, 0, len(modifiedFiles))
   428→						for _, f := range modifiedFiles {
   429→							if rel := paths.ToRelativePath(f, basePath); rel != "" {
   430→								normalized = append(normalized, rel)
   431→							} else {
   432→								normalized = append(normalized, f)
   433→							}
   434→						}
   435→						modifiedFiles = normalized
   436→					}
   437→					data.FilesTouched = modifiedFiles
   438→				}
   439→			}
   440→		}
   441→	}
   442→
   443→	// Calculate token usage from the extracted transcript portion
   444→	if len(data.Transcript) > 0 {
   445→		data.TokenUsage = calculateTokenUsage(state.AgentType, data.Transcript, state.CheckpointTranscriptStart)
   446→	}
   447→

> AGENT

Found another gap. `extractSessionDataFromLiveTranscript` at line 417 also only uses `analyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset` — no subagent extraction. This is the function that builds `FilesTouched` for the condensation data when there's no shadow branch. So even if `hasNew` is true, the condensed checkpoint would have empty `FilesTouched`.

But actually, looking back at the user's logs again more carefully — there's **no** PostCommit log entry at all (not even WARN or INFO level). The `session condensed` INFO log that `condenseAndUpdateState` emits (line 749) never appears. The `post-commit: no active sessions despite trailer` WARN (line 504) never appears either.

This suggests PostCommit might not be running at all, or it's hitting one of the silent `return nil` early exits (lines 473, 479, 484).

Actually wait — the hook logs are at DEBUG level (`logInvoked`). Let me re-examine. The only INFO-level log from PostCommit is the `session condensed` message. If PostCommit ran but `hasNew` was false, there'd be no INFO log at all — it would just update BaseCommit silently at line 616.

So the actual sequence is most likely:
1. Subagent commits → prepare-commit-msg adds trailer
2. PostCommit runs → `hasNew = false` (our bug, now fixed) → no condensation, just updates BaseCommit
3. Stop hook → creates shadow branch checkpoint (StepCount = 1)
4. Turn end → IDLE, no condensation because there's no commit happening

The checkpoint trailer `71714d3bd362` on the user commit points to nothing because condensation was skipped. Our fix to `sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles` should address this. But there's also the `extractSessionDataFromLiveTranscript` gap (line 417) which would affect the `FilesTouched` in the condensed data even after our fix.

> AGENT

Good, that's a nice -28 lines.

Re: your note about log levels — that makes sense, the PostCommit hooks all log at DEBUG level by default. With DEBUG enabled you should see the `post-commit hook invoked` / `post-commit hook completed` messages plus the detailed content-check logs which will reveal exactly where it bails out.

Here's a summary of the debugging analysis and what we found:

**Root cause of "trailer added but no checkpoint":**

The subagent commits mid-turn → PostCommit runs with ACTIVE session where `StepCount == 0 && FilesTouched == 0` → falls through to `sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles` → this only checked the main transcript (no subagent files) → returned `false` → no condensation → checkpoint trailer `71714d3bd362` points to nothing.

**Three fixes applied:**

1. **`sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles`** (PostCommit content detection) — now uses shared helper with subagent support
2. **`extractFilesFromLiveTranscript`** (PostCommit carry-forward) — now uses shared helper with subagent support  
3. **`extractSessionDataFromLiveTranscript`** (condensation data extraction) — now uses shared helper with subagent support, also cleaned up 28 lines of duplicated extraction+normalization code

All three were calling `analyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset` directly instead of going through `extractModifiedFilesFromLiveTranscript` which includes the subagent check.

> AGENT

Now I can see the problem clearly from the DEBUG logs. Look at this sequence:

```
15:23:48 post-commit hook invoked
15:23:48 phase unchanged: active
15:23:48 carry-forward prep: files_touched_before=0, files=null
15:23:48 updated BaseCommit → a26f0d1
15:23:48 post-commit hook completed
```

PostCommit ran but **skipped condensation**. `files_touched_before=0` means `extractFilesFromLiveTranscript` found nothing. And the `hasNew` check also returned false, going straight to `updateBaseCommitIfChanged`.

The root cause isn't the subagent extraction code — it's a **timing issue**. At PostCommit time (15:23:48), the subagent is still running (it started at 15:23:28 and finishes at 15:23:53). The main transcript has a `tool_use` for the Task but no `tool_result` yet (subagent hasn't returned). So `ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs` finds nothing — there's no `agentId:` in a tool_result to parse. And even if we could identify the subagent, its transcript file might not exist yet.

The transcript-based detection fundamentally can't work for mid-subagent commits.

The fix is straightforward: for ACTIVE sessions, trust the trailer. The comment at line 538 already says this:

> "For ACTIVE sessions: the commit has a checkpoint trailer (verified above), meaning PrepareCommitMsg already determined this commit is session-related. We trust that and assume hasNew = true"

But the code contradicts the comment by still checking `StepCount > 0 || len(FilesTouched) > 0` first. Let me fix it.

> AGENT

Right — `ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs` only finds agents from `tool_result` entries, which don't exist until the subagent finishes. So this can't work at PostCommit time.

But `FindActivePreTaskFile()` is a concrete, reliable signal that a subagent is running right now. Here's my thinking:

**Available evidence at PostCommit time:**
1. Pre-task file exists → subagent is actively running
2. `committedFileSet` is non-empty → the commit actually has files  
3. Session is ACTIVE → agent is in the middle of a turn
4. Commit has a checkpoint trailer → our prepare-commit-msg hook added it

The pre-task file is the most specific signal. A pre-task file existing + files in the commit = a subagent made this commit. Rather than blindly trusting all ACTIVE sessions, we could use the pre-task file as the tiebreaker when `StepCount == 0 && FilesTouched == 0`.

What do you think — use the pre-task file as the fallback evidence, or is there a simpler signal you'd prefer?

> DEVELOPER

the alternative is that the human has made some changes yeah? can we tell if those changes preceded the userPromptSubmit?

    43→// "untracked_files": null), returns an empty non-nil slice so that all current untracked files
    44→// are correctly treated as new.
    45→func (s *PrePromptState) PreUntrackedFiles() []string {
    46→	if s == nil {
    47→		return nil
    48→	}
    49→	if s.UntrackedFiles == nil {
    50→		return []string{}
    51→	}
    52→	return s.UntrackedFiles
    53→}
    54→
    55→// normalizePrePromptState migrates deprecated fields after loading from JSON.
    56→func (s *PrePromptState) normalizePrePromptState() {
    57→	if s.StepTranscriptStart == 0 && s.LastTranscriptLineCount > 0 {
    58→		s.StepTranscriptStart = s.LastTranscriptLineCount
    59→	}
    60→	s.LastTranscriptLineCount = 0
    61→}
    62→
    63→// CapturePrePromptState captures current untracked files and transcript position before a prompt
    64→// and saves them to a state file.
    65→// Works correctly from any subdirectory within the repository.
    66→// The transcriptPath parameter is optional - if empty, transcript position won't be captured.
    67→func CapturePrePromptState(sessionID, transcriptPath string) error {
    68→	if sessionID == "" {
    69→		sessionID = unknownSessionID
    70→	}
    71→
    72→	// Get absolute path for tmp directory
    73→	tmpDirAbs, err := paths.AbsPath(paths.EntireTmpDir)
    74→	if err != nil {
    75→		tmpDirAbs = paths.EntireTmpDir // Fallback to relative
    76→	}
    77→
    78→	// Create tmp directory if it doesn't exist
    79→	if err := os.MkdirAll(tmpDirAbs, 0o750); err != nil {
    80→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create tmp directory: %w", err)
    81→	}
    82→
    83→	// Get list of untracked files (excluding .entire directory itself)
    84→	untrackedFiles, err := getUntrackedFilesForState()
    85→	if err != nil {
    86→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to get untracked files: %w", err)
    87→	}
    88→
    89→	// Get transcript position (last UUID and line count)
    90→	var transcriptPos TranscriptPosition
    91→	if transcriptPath != "" {
    92→		transcriptPos, err = GetTranscriptPosition(transcriptPath)
    93→		if err != nil {
    94→			// Log warning but don't fail - transcript position is optional
    95→			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to get transcript position: %v\n", err)
    96→		}
    97→	}
    98→
    99→	// Create state file
   100→	stateFile := prePromptStateFile(sessionID)
   101→	state := PrePromptState{
   102→		SessionID:                sessionID,
   103→		Timestamp:                time.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339),
   104→		UntrackedFiles:           untrackedFiles,
   105→		LastTranscriptIdentifier: transcriptPos.LastUUID,
   106→		StepTranscriptStart:      transcriptPos.LineCount,
   107→	}
   108→
   109→	data, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(state, "", "  ")
   110→	if err != nil {
   111→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal state: %w", err)
   112→	}
   113→

> AGENT

We only capture `UntrackedFiles` — not modified/staged files. So we don't have a pre-session snapshot of the working tree's dirty state.

But we don't need one. We can compare `committedFileSet` against the tree at `state.BaseCommit` (which is HEAD at session start). If a file in the commit was **already different** from `state.BaseCommit`'s tree before the agent started, it's a pre-existing human change. If the file was identical to `state.BaseCommit` (i.e., the diff was introduced during the session), it must be agent/subagent work.

Actually, even simpler: `state.BaseCommit` is the parent of this commit (updated by PostCommit). The commit diff itself tells us files were changed. The question is whether those changes existed before `UserPromptSubmit` or were introduced during the session. But at this point in PostCommit, the commit is already made — the files are in the commit. We can't distinguish pre-existing dirty state from agent-introduced changes just from the commit.

However — we CAN check the pre-task file. That's a much cleaner signal:

- **Pre-task file exists** → subagent is running → subagent likely made this commit → `hasNew = true`
- **No pre-task file** → no subagent → main agent or human made the commit → fall through to transcript check (which would succeed for main agent, or show no agent files for human)

This is more targeted than blindly trusting all ACTIVE sessions. What do you think?

> AGENT

There it is. Line 275-281:

```go
if !hasTTY() {
    for _, state := range sessions {
        if state.Phase.IsActive() {
            return s.addTrailerForAgentCommit(...)
        }
    }
}
```

The fast path: **no TTY + ACTIVE session** → blindly add trailer. No content detection. The logic is: if there's no terminal, the agent (or subagent) must be making this commit, so just trust it.

The slow path (lines 284+): TTY exists → `filterSessionsWithNewContent` → content detection → interactive prompt.

So the prepare-commit-msg decides based on `!hasTTY()`. When a subagent runs `git commit`, there's no TTY, so the fast path fires and adds the trailer unconditionally.

PostCommit then needs to match this logic. If the trailer was added via the fast path (`!hasTTY()` + ACTIVE), PostCommit should trust that same signal. But PostCommit doesn't have `hasTTY()` — by the time PostCommit runs, the commit is done and we're in a git hook context regardless.

The key insight: the trailer itself is the signal. If it was added to an ACTIVE session, it came from the no-TTY fast path (agent/subagent commit). The slow path (human commit with TTY) goes through content detection first. So in PostCommit, for an ACTIVE session with a trailer, the prepare-commit-msg already vouched for this commit.

> DEVELOPER

so ACTIVE AND trailer exists on commit => condense?

the current behaviour exists to try and filter pure human commits right? so if the trailer is already on there...

> AGENT

Exactly. The trailer is the gatekeeper. It only gets added to an ACTIVE session via:
- **No TTY** → agent/subagent is committing → added unconditionally
- **TTY** → `filterSessionsWithNewContent` already confirmed agent work → added after content detection

Either way, the prepare-commit-msg already validated this commit. The `StepCount/FilesTouched` check in PostCommit is re-validating something that was already decided. And the transcript-based fallback breaks for mid-subagent commits where the data isn't available yet.

So yes: ACTIVE + trailer exists → `hasNew = true`. Simple.

   936→// the committed files. This is used in PostCommit for ACTIVE sessions where staged files

   941→// If the agent makes file modifications via shell commands (e.g., `sed`, `mv`, `cp`) that

   943→// not be detected. This is an acceptable edge case because:

This is used in PostCommit for ACTIVE sessions where staged files\n// are empty (already committed). Uses the live transcript to extract modified files and\n// compares against the committed file set.\n//\n// KNOWN LIMITATION: This function relies on transcript analysis to detect file modifications.\n// If the agent makes file modifications via shell commands (e.g., `sed`, `mv`, `cp`) that\n// aren't captured in the transcript's file modification tracking, those modifications may\n// not be detected. This is an acceptable edge case because:\n// 1. Most agent file modifications use the Write/Edit tools which are tracked\n// 2. Shell-based modifications are relatively rare in practice\n// 3. The consequence (missing a checkpoint trailer) is minor - the transcript is still saved\nfunc (s *ManualCommitStrategy) sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles(state *SessionState, committedFiles map[string]struct{}) bool {\n\tlogCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), \"checkpoint\")\n\n\tmodifiedFiles, ok := s.extractNewModifiedFilesFromLiveTranscript(state)\n\tif !ok || len(modifiedFiles) == 0 {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\n\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"committed files check: found file modifications\",\n\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t\tslog.Int(\"modified_files\", len(modifiedFiles)),\n\t\tslog.Int(\"committed_files\", len(committedFiles)),\n\t)\n\n\t// Check if any modified files overlap with committed files\n\tfor _, f := range modifiedFiles {\n\t\tif _, ok := committedFiles[f]; ok {\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"committed files check: no overlap between committed and modified files\",\n\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t)\n\treturn false\n}\n\n// extractFilesFromLiveTranscript extracts modified file paths from the live transcript.",
  "new_string": "// extractFilesFromLiveTranscript extracts modified file paths from the live transcript."
}

   176→// the shadow branch is preserved when an ACTIVE session that was NOT condensed
   177→// (e.g., it has no new content) still shares the branch with another session.
   178→// Both sessions condense immediately on GitCommit, but the branch is only deleted
   179→// when ALL sessions on it have been condensed.
   180→func TestPostCommit_ShadowBranch_PreservedWhenUncondensedActiveSessionExists(t *testing.T) {
   181→	dir := setupGitRepo(t)
   182→	t.Chdir(dir)
   183→
   184→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(dir)
   185→	require.NoError(t, err)
   186→
   187→	s := &ManualCommitStrategy{}
   188→	idleSessionID := "test-postcommit-idle-multi"
   189→	activeSessionID := "test-postcommit-active-multi"
   190→
   191→	// Initialize the idle session with a checkpoint
   192→	setupSessionWithCheckpoint(t, s, repo, dir, idleSessionID)
   193→
   194→	// Get worktree path and base commit from the idle session
   195→	idleState, err := s.loadSessionState(idleSessionID)
   196→	require.NoError(t, err)
   197→	worktreePath := idleState.WorktreePath
   198→	baseCommit := idleState.BaseCommit
   199→	worktreeID := idleState.WorktreeID
   200→
   201→	// Set idle session to IDLE phase
   202→	idleState.Phase = session.PhaseIdle
   203→	idleState.LastInteractionTime = nil
   204→	idleState.FilesTouched = []string{"test.txt"}
   205→	require.NoError(t, s.saveSessionState(idleState))
   206→
   207→	// Create a second session with the SAME base commit and worktree (concurrent session).
   208→	// This session is ACTIVE but has NO checkpoints (StepCount=0, no shadow branch content).
   209→	// Because it has no new content, it will NOT be condensed, and its shadow branch must
   210→	// be preserved so it can save checkpoints later.
   211→	now := time.Now()
   212→	activeState := &SessionState{
   213→		SessionID:           activeSessionID,
   214→		BaseCommit:          baseCommit,
   215→		WorktreePath:        worktreePath,
   216→		WorktreeID:          worktreeID,
   217→		StartedAt:           now,
   218→		Phase:               session.PhaseActive,
   219→		LastInteractionTime: &now,
   220→		StepCount:           0,
   221→	}
   222→	require.NoError(t, s.saveSessionState(activeState))
   223→
   224→	// Record shadow branch name before PostCommit
   225→	shadowBranch := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(baseCommit, worktreeID)
   226→

   228→	commitWithCheckpointTrailer(t, repo, dir, "d4e5f6a1b2c3")

   231→	err = s.PostCommit()
   232→	require.NoError(t, err)
   233→
   234→	// Verify the ACTIVE session stays ACTIVE (immediate condensation model)
   235→	activeState, err = s.loadSessionState(activeSessionID)
   236→	require.NoError(t, err)
   237→	assert.Equal(t, session.PhaseActive, activeState.Phase,
   238→		"ACTIVE session should stay ACTIVE after GitCommit")
   239→
   240→	// Verify the IDLE session actually condensed (entire/checkpoints/v1 branch should exist)
   241→	idleState, err = s.loadSessionState(idleSessionID)
   242→	require.NoError(t, err)
   243→	sessionsRef, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName), true)
   244→	require.NoError(t, err, "entire/checkpoints/v1 branch should exist after IDLE session condensation")
   245→	require.NotNil(t, sessionsRef)
   246→

   248→	assert.Equal(t, 0, idleState.StepCount,

   250→

   257→}

> AGENT

This test was designed for the old behavior where an ACTIVE session with no content would skip condensation. With our fix, ACTIVE sessions always condense (trusting the trailer), so the shadow branch is correctly cleaned up. Let me update the test to reflect the new expected behavior.

Initialize the idle session with a checkpoint\n\tsetupSessionWithCheckpoint(t, s, repo, dir, idleSessionID)\n\n\t// Get worktree path and base commit from the idle session\n\tidleState, err := s.loadSessionState(idleSessionID)\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\tworktreePath := idleState.WorktreePath\n\tbaseCommit := idleState.BaseCommit\n\tworktreeID := idleState.WorktreeID\n\n\t// Set idle session to IDLE phase\n\tidleState.Phase = session.PhaseIdle\n\tidleState.LastInteractionTime = nil\n\tidleState.FilesTouched = []string{\"test.txt\"}\n\trequire.NoError(t, s.saveSessionState(idleState))\n\n\t// Create a second session with the SAME base commit and worktree (concurrent session).\n\t// This session is ACTIVE but has NO checkpoints (StepCount=0, no shadow branch content).\n\t// Because it has no new content, it will NOT be condensed, and its shadow branch must\n\t// be preserved so it can save checkpoints later.\n\tnow := time.Now()\n\tactiveState := reset after condensation\")\n\n\t// Verify shadow branch is preserved because the ACTIVE session was not condensed\n\t// (it had no new content) and still needs the branch for future uncondensed active session still exists on it\")\n}",

s.saveSessionState(idleState))\n\n\t// Create a second session with the SAME base commit and worktree (concurrent session).\n\t// This session is ACTIVE but has NO checkpoints (StepCount=0, no shadow branch content).\n\t// Despite having no content, it WILL be condensed because ACTIVE sessions always\n\t// condense — PrepareCommitMsg already validated the trailer.\n\tnow := time.Now()\n\tactiveState := &SessionState{\n\t\tSessionID:           activeSessionID,\n\t\tBaseCommit:          baseCommit,\n\t\tWorktreePath:        worktreePath,\n\t\tWorktreeID: s.saveSessionState(activeState))\n\n\t// Record shadow branch name before PostCommit\n\tshadowBranch := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(baseCommit, worktreeID)\n\n\t// Create a commit WITH the checkpoint trailer\n\tcommitWithCheckpointTrailer(t, repo, dir, \"d4e5f6a1b2c3\")\n\n\t// Run PostCommit\n\terr = s.PostCommit()\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\n\t// Verify the ACTIVE session stays ACTIVE (immediate condensation model)\n\tactiveState, err = s.loadSessionState(activeSessionID)\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\tassert.Equal(t, session.PhaseActive, activeState.Phase,\n\t\t\"ACTIVE session should stay ACTIVE after GitCommit\")\n\n\t// Verify both sessions condensed (entire/checkpoints/v1 branch should exist)\n\tidleState, err = s.loadSessionState(idleSessionID)\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\tsessionsRef, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName), true)\n\trequire.NoError(t, err, \"entire/checkpoints/v1 branch should exist after condensation\")\n\trequire.NotNil(t, sessionsRef)\n\n\t// Verify IDLE session's StepCount was reset by condensation\n\tassert.Equal(t, 0, idleState.StepCount,\n\t\t\"IDLE session StepCount should be reset after condensation\")\n\n\t// Verify shadow branch is cleaned up because ALL sessions condensed\n\t// (both IDLE and ACTIVE were condensed on this commit)\n\trefName := 3. **Updated stale comment** referencing the removed function
4. **Updated test** `TestPostCommit_ActiveSessionAlwaysCondenses` to reflect the new behavior: all sessions condense, shadow branch is cleaned up

Ready for you to re-test manually. You'll want to push first — there are now 3 local commits ahead of origin.

> AGENT

So the structure is:

```
<transcriptDir>/<sessionID>/subagents/agent-<agentID>.jsonl
```

Currently `ExtractAllModifiedFiles` finds subagent IDs by parsing the main transcript for `agentId:` in tool_results. But yes — the subagent transcript files are written to disk as soon as the subagent starts running, well before the main transcript has the `tool_result`.

So instead of:
1. Parse main transcript → find `agentId: xyz` in tool_results → read `agent-xyz.jsonl`

We could:
1. Glob `<transcriptDir>/<sessionID>/subagents/agent-*.jsonl` → read all files found

This would detect subagent activity even mid-execution, which means we could go back to content-based detection for ACTIVE sessions instead of the blanket `hasNew = true`.

The false positive you described — human changes + agent commit, no subagent work — would be avoided because we'd see no subagent transcript files on disk and the main transcript wouldn't show file modifications either.

Let me check `ExtractAllModifiedFiles` to see how we'd modify it.

   431→// ExtractAllModifiedFiles extracts files modified by both the main agent and
   432→// any subagents spawned via the Task tool. It parses the main transcript from
   433→// startLine, collects modified files from the main agent, then reads each
   434→// subagent's transcript from subagentsDir to collect their modified files too.
   435→// The result is a deduplicated list of all modified file paths.
   436→func ExtractAllModifiedFiles(transcriptPath string, startLine int, subagentsDir string) ([]string, error) {
   437→	if transcriptPath == "" {
   438→		return nil, nil
   439→	}
   440→
   441→	// Parse main transcript once
   442→	transcript, err := parseTranscriptFromLine(transcriptPath, startLine)
   443→	if err != nil {
   444→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse transcript: %w", err)
   445→	}
   446→
   447→	// Collect modified files from main agent
   448→	fileSet := make(map[string]bool)
   449→	var files []string
   450→	for _, f := range ExtractModifiedFiles(transcript) {
   451→		if !fileSet[f] {
   452→			fileSet[f] = true
   453→			files = append(files, f)
   454→		}
   455→	}
   456→
   457→	// Find spawned subagents and collect their modified files
   458→	agentIDs := ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs(transcript)
   459→	for agentID := range agentIDs {
   460→		agentPath := filepath.Join(subagentsDir, fmt.Sprintf("agent-%s.jsonl", agentID))
   461→		agentTranscript, err := parseTranscriptFromLine(agentPath, 0)
   462→		if err != nil {
   463→			// Subagent transcript may not exist yet or may have been cleaned up
   464→			continue
   465→		}
   466→		for _, f := range ExtractModifiedFiles(agentTranscript) {
   467→			if !fileSet[f] {
   468→				fileSet[f] = true
   469→				files = append(files, f)
   470→			}
   471→		}
   472→	}
   473→
   474→	return files, nil
   475→}

   393→	}
   394→
   395→	// Parse transcript ONCE

   399→	}

   403→

   405→	agentIDs := ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs(transcript)
   406→

   418→			subagentUsage.CacheCreationTokens += agentUsage.CacheCreationTokens
   419→			subagentUsage.CacheReadTokens += agentUsage.CacheReadTokens
   420→			subagentUsage.OutputTokens += agentUsage.OutputTokens
   421→			subagentUsage.APICallCount += agentUsage.APICallCount
   422→		}
   423→		if subagentUsage.APICallCount > 0 {
   424→			mainUsage.SubagentTokens = subagentUsage
   425→		}
   426→	}
   427→
   428→	return mainUsage, nil

> AGENT

Both functions have the same pattern. Here's the approach:

We could extract a helper like `discoverSubagentTranscripts(subagentsDir) []string` that globs `agent-*.jsonl` in the directory. Then use that as the primary discovery mechanism — it catches subagents the moment they start writing, regardless of whether the main transcript has recorded their completion.

The `ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs` approach becomes a subset — it only finds subagents whose `tool_result` is already in the main transcript. The glob finds all of them.

This would let us revert the blanket `hasNew = true` for ACTIVE sessions back to content-based detection, because we'd actually be able to detect subagent file modifications reliably.

Want me to implement this? The changes would be:

1. Add `discoverSubagentTranscripts(subagentsDir string) []string` — globs `agent-*.jsonl`
2. Update `ExtractAllModifiedFiles` to use glob instead of (or in addition to) `ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs`
3. Same for `CalculateTotalTokenUsage`
4. Revert the blanket `hasNew = true` in PostCommit back to content-based detection (which now works because glob catches mid-execution subagents)

> DEVELOPER

and we're guaranteed that these subagents belong to the main session yeah? but the tricky part will be identifying which subagents are currently live, as older subagent logs might be in there with file changes...

> AGENT

Right, that's the key issue. The session directory persists across turns, so after condensation you'd still have old subagent files from previous turns sitting alongside new ones.

The current `ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs` from `startLine` handles this correctly for *completed* subagents — it only finds `agentId:` references in the post-condensation portion of the main transcript. But it misses *running* subagents.

To make the glob approach work, you'd need to distinguish new from old. Something like:

1. Glob `agent-*.jsonl` → finds {A, B, C}
2. `ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs` from full transcript (offset 0) → {A, B} (all *completed* subagents ever)
3. Diff: {C} = on disk but not in any `tool_result` → currently running, therefore current turn

That works because a subagent can only be "on disk but not in the transcript" if it's *currently executing*. Once it completes, the `tool_result` gets written.

But honestly — is the false positive actually a problem? Think about the scenario again:

- Agent is ACTIVE, running subagents
- Human makes changes in another terminal
- Human asks agent to commit → **agent runs `git commit`** → no TTY → trailer added unconditionally

That's an agent-driven commit during an active session. Including a checkpoint seems... correct? The session is active, the agent is orchestrating the commit.

The other case — human opens a separate terminal and runs `git commit` while agent is ACTIVE — goes through the TTY path in PrepareCommitMsg, which does content detection *before* adding the trailer. So the blanket `hasNew = true` in PostCommit is already gated behind "trailer exists."

The false positive window seems narrow enough that the complexity of glob + diff might not be worth it. What do you think — is there a concrete scenario you're worried about?

> DEVELOPER

we read the commit message and trailer in the post-commit hook yeah?

> AGENT

Yes — let me confirm the exact check.

So the gate is already there: **no trailer → no condensation**. The `hasNew = true` for ACTIVE sessions at line 547-548 only runs if the trailer was present on the commit.

Is your thinking: since we already confirmed the trailer exists, maybe the ACTIVE `hasNew = true` is fine and the false positive concern is moot? The trailer is the real signal — PrepareCommitMsg already did the validation work.

   560→		transitionCtx.HasFilesTouched = len(state.FilesTouched) > 0

   562→		// Run the state machine transition
   563→		remaining := TransitionAndLog(state, session.EventGitCommit, transitionCtx)
   564→
   565→		// Save FilesTouched BEFORE the action loop — condensation clears it,
   566→		// but we need the original list for carry-forward computation.
   567→		// For mid-session commits (ACTIVE, no shadow branch), state.FilesTouched may be empty
   568→		// because no SaveChanges/Stop has been called yet. Extract files from transcript.
   569→		filesTouchedBefore := make([]string, len(state.FilesTouched))
   570→		copy(filesTouchedBefore, state.FilesTouched)

   580→			slog.Any("files", filesTouchedBefore),

   583→		condensed := false
   584→
   585→		// Dispatch strategy-specific actions.
   586→		// Each branch handles its own BaseCommit update so there is no
   587→		// fallthrough conditional at the end. On condensation failure,
   588→		// BaseCommit is intentionally NOT updated to preserve access to
   589→		// the shadow branch (which is named after the old BaseCommit).
   590→		for _, action := range remaining {
   591→			switch action {
   592→			case session.ActionCondense:
   593→				// For ACTIVE sessions, any commit during the turn is session-related.
   594→				// For IDLE/ENDED sessions (e.g., carry-forward), also require that the
   595→				// committed files overlap with the session's remaining files AND have
   596→				// matching content — otherwise an unrelated commit (or a commit with
   597→				// completely replaced content) would incorrectly get this session's checkpoint.
   598→				shouldCondense := hasNew

   600→					shouldCondense = filesOverlapWithContent(repo, shadowBranchName, commit, state.FilesTouched)
   601→				}
   602→				if shouldCondense {
   603→					condensed = s.condenseAndUpdateState(logCtx, repo, checkpointID, state, head, shadowBranchName, shadowBranchesToDelete)
   604→					// condenseAndUpdateState updates BaseCommit on success.
   605→					// On failure, BaseCommit is preserved so the shadow branch remains accessible.
   606→				} else {
   607→					// No new content or unrelated commit — just update BaseCommit
   608→					s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(logCtx, state, newHead)
   609→				}
   610→			case session.ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched:

   612→				// but hasNew is an additional content-level check (transcript has
   613→				// new content beyond what was previously condensed).
   614→				if len(state.FilesTouched) > 0 && hasNew {
   615→					condensed = s.condenseAndUpdateState(logCtx, repo, checkpointID, state, head, shadowBranchName, shadowBranchesToDelete)
   616→					// On failure, BaseCommit is preserved (same as ActionCondense).
   617→				} else {
   618→					s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(logCtx, state, newHead)
   619→				}
   620→			case session.ActionDiscardIfNoFiles:
   621→				if len(state.FilesTouched) == 0 {
   622→					logging.Debug(logCtx, "post-commit: skipping empty ended session (no files to condense)",
   623→						slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   624→					)
   625→				}
   626→				s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(logCtx, state, newHead)

   634→		// Record checkpoint ID for ACTIVE sessions so HandleTurnEnd can finalize
   635→		// with full transcript. IDLE/ENDED sessions already have complete transcripts.
   636→		if condensed && state.Phase.IsActive() {
   637→			state.TurnCheckpointIDs = append(state.TurnCheckpointIDs, checkpointID.String())
   638→		}
   639→
   640→		// Carry forward remaining uncommitted files so the next commit gets its
   641→		// own checkpoint ID. This applies to ALL phases — if a user splits their
   642→		// commit across two `git commit` invocations, each gets a 1:1 checkpoint.
   643→		// Uses content-aware comparison: if user did `git add -p` and committed
   644→		// partial changes, the file still has remaining agent changes to carry forward.
   645→		if condensed {
   646→			remainingFiles := filesWithRemainingAgentChanges(repo, shadowBranchName, commit, filesTouchedBefore, committedFileSet)
   647→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "post-commit: carry-forward decision (content-aware)",
   648→				slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   649→				slog.Int("files_touched_before", len(filesTouchedBefore)),
   650→				slog.Int("committed_files", len(committedFileSet)),
   651→				slog.Int("remaining_files", len(remainingFiles)),
   652→				slog.Any("remaining", remainingFiles),
   653→			)
   654→			if len(remainingFiles) > 0 {
   655→				s.carryForwardToNewShadowBranch(logCtx, repo, state, remainingFiles)
   656→			}
   657→		}
   658→
   659→		// Save the updated state
   660→		if err := s.saveSessionState(state); err != nil {
   661→			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Warning: failed to update session state: %v\n", err)
   662→		}
   663→
   664→		// Only preserve shadow branch for active sessions that were NOT condensed.
   665→		// Condensed sessions already have their data on entire/checkpoints/v1.
   666→		if state.Phase.IsActive() && !condensed {
   667→			uncondensedActiveOnBranch[shadowBranchName] = true
   668→		}
   669→	}
   670→
   671→	// Clean up shadow branches — only delete when ALL sessions on the branch are non-active
   672→	// or were condensed during this PostCommit.
   673→	for shadowBranchName := range shadowBranchesToDelete {
   674→		if uncondensedActiveOnBranch[shadowBranchName] {
   675→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "post-commit: preserving shadow branch (active session exists)",
   676→				slog.String("shadow_branch", shadowBranchName),
   677→			)
   678→			continue
```

> AGENT

Ah, I see. `hasNew` serves two distinct purposes:

1. **ACTIVE sessions** (line 598): `shouldCondense = hasNew` — we just set this to `true` unconditionally now
2. **IDLE/ENDED sessions** (lines 599-601, 614): `hasNew` is still meaningful — it prevents condensing a stale session that has no new content since its last condensation

The multi-session scenario matters: say you have two sessions on the same worktree:
- Session A (IDLE, has new content) → caused the trailer to be added
- Session B (IDLE, no new content since last condensation) → just sitting there

Without `hasNew`, session B would get condensed with empty content on every commit session A triggers. Harmless but wasteful, and it would create noise in the checkpoint metadata.

So `hasNew` is still useful for **non-ACTIVE** sessions. For ACTIVE sessions, it's now always `true` — which is what you're pointing at. We could simplify by removing the variable for the ACTIVE path and just inlining `true`, but functionally it's the same as what we already committed.

The real question you were driving at earlier — could we be smarter about ACTIVE sessions using filesystem-based subagent detection — that would let us distinguish "agent is actually doing work" from "agent is idle but phase is ACTIVE." But given the trailer gate, I'm not sure there's a practical false positive left to worry about.

> DEVELOPER

oh, hasNew is a session specific flag

> AGENT

Right. The trailer is per-commit ("at least one session had content"), but `hasNew` is per-session ("does *this* session have content to condense"). Multiple sessions can be on the same worktree, and only some may have new work.

So the blanket `hasNew = true` for ACTIVE sessions means: if there are two concurrent ACTIVE sessions and only one did real work, both get condensed — the other produces an empty condensation. Harmless but slightly wasteful.

That said, concurrent ACTIVE sessions where one has zero content seems like a pretty narrow edge case. And the empty condensation just means 0 checkpoints / 0 transcript lines in the metadata — no data loss, just a no-op entry.

I think the current approach is a reasonable tradeoff. The filesystem-based subagent detection would make it more precise, but the complexity of handling stale subagent logs (comparing glob results against `ExtractSpawnedAgentIDs` from the full transcript) doesn't seem justified for avoiding empty condensations in a rare multi-session scenario.

Want to push what we have and re-test, or do you want to refine further?

> DEVELOPER

{
  "command": "gh pr edit 323 --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\nFixes subagent file modifications being invisible to checkpoint detection. When Claude Code delegates work to subagents via the Task tool, file changes made by those subagents were not tracked, causing:\n- Missing checkpoints at Stop (subagent-only changes → `totalChanges=0`)\n- Missing checkpoint trailers on mid-session commits\n- Incomplete file lists in condensed checkpoint metadata\n- Failed PostCommit condensation for mid-subagent commits (transcript data unavailable)\n\n### Changes\n\n- **`ExtractAllModifiedFiles()`** — new function that scans both main and subagent transcripts (`agent-<id>.jsonl`) for Write/Edit/NotebookEdit tool calls\n- **Stop hook** — uses `ExtractAllModifiedFiles` so checkpoints aren't skipped when only subagents modify files\n- **`sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript`** — augmented with subagent transcript scanning for mid-session commit detection\n- **Shared helper `extractModifiedFilesFromLiveTranscript`** — consolidates 3 duplicated code blocks (main transcript extraction + subagent augmentation + path normalization)\n- **`extractSessionDataFromLiveTranscript`** — uses shared helper so condensed checkpoints include subagent files\n- **PostCommit ACTIVE sessions** — trusts PrepareCommitMsg's trailer validation instead of re-analyzing transcripts (unreliable when subagents are still running at commit time)\n- **Removed `sessionHasNewContentInCommittedFiles`** — now unused after the PostCommit simplification\n\n### Key insight\n\nAt PostCommit time during a mid-subagent commit, subagent transcript data is unavailable: the main transcript has no `tool_result` with `agentId:` yet, and subagent files may still be in-flight. Since PrepareCommitMsg already validates the trailer (unconditionally for no-TTY/agent commits, via content detection for TTY/human commits), PostCommit trusts that decision for ACTIVE sessions rather than re-validating.\n\nFixes [ENT-297](https://linear.app/entirehq/issue/ENT-297)\n\n## Test plan\n- [x] Unit tests for `ExtractAllModifiedFiles` (4 cases: includes subagent files, deduplicates, no subagents, subagent-only changes)\n- [x] Unit test for `sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript` with subagent-only modifications\n- [x] Unit test for PostCommit ACTIVE session always condensing\n- [x] Integration test `TestHookRunner_SimulateStop_SubagentOnlyChanges` (both strategies)\n- [x] Manual test: subagent commit → trailer added → checkpoint created\n- [x] Full CI suite passes (`mise run fmt && mise run lint && mise run test:ci`)\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Update PR description"
}

> AGENT

It's a valid point. For Claude Code agents, `ExtractAllModifiedFiles` at line 1016 re-parses the same transcript that `analyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset` already parsed at line 1004. Since `ExtractAllModifiedFiles` returns main + subagent files as a superset, the first parse is redundant for Claude Code.

For non-Claude Code agents, only the `analyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset` path runs. So the fix is to skip line 1004 when we know we'll call `ExtractAllModifiedFiles` anyway.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01WFJA7PfzexsRQNwsEPvb8k
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/alex/workspace/cli/.worktrees/2/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go",
  "old_string": "\tmodifiedFiles, _, err := analyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(state.TranscriptPath, offset)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"extractModifiedFilesFromLiveTranscript: main transcript extraction failed\",\n\t\t\tslog.String(\"transcript_path\", state.TranscriptPath),\n\t\t\tslog.Any(\"error\", err),\n\t\t)\n\t}\n\n\t// Also check subagent transcripts (Claude Code specific).\n\t// Subagents spawned via the Task tool write to separate transcript files.\n\tif state.AgentType == agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode {\n\t\tsubagentsDir := filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(state.TranscriptPath), state.SessionID, \"subagents\")\n\t\tallFiles, extractErr := claudecode.ExtractAllModifiedFiles(state.TranscriptPath, offset, subagentsDir)\n\t\tif extractErr != nil {\n\t\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"extractModifiedFilesFromLiveTranscript: subagent extraction failed\",\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"error\", extractErr.Error()),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t} else if len(allFiles) > len(modifiedFiles) {\n\t\t\tmodifiedFiles = allFiles\n\t\t}\n\t}",
  "new_string": "\tvar modifiedFiles []string\n\n\t// For Claude Code, use ExtractAllModifiedFiles which parses the main transcript\n\t// AND subagent transcripts in a single pass, avoiding redundant parsing.\n\tif state.AgentType == agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode {\n\t\tsubagentsDir := filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(state.TranscriptPath), state.SessionID, \"subagents\")\n\t\tallFiles, extractErr := claudecode.ExtractAllModifiedFiles(state.TranscriptPath, offset, subagentsDir)\n\t\tif extractErr != nil {\n\t\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"extractModifiedFilesFromLiveTranscript: extraction failed\",\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"session_id\", state.SessionID),\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"error\", extractErr.Error()),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tmodifiedFiles = allFiles\n\t\t}\n\t} else {\n\t\tfiles, err := analyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(state.TranscriptPath, offset)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"extractModifiedFilesFromLiveTranscript: main transcript extraction failed\",\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"transcript_path\", state.TranscriptPath),\n\t\t\t\tslog.Any(\"error\", err),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tmodifiedFiles = files\n\t\t}\n\t}"
}
